# RS3 duo switchover parity — 2026-10-08

This is the **pre-repair baseline** at audit commit `89cc2f3`. Present-tense coverage and metadata descriptions below refer to that audited snapshot. The [repair verification](switchover-parity-fixes-2026-10-08.md) and [processing follow-up](switchover-processing-parity-2026-10-08.md) supersede its blocker statuses, parity counts and progress-alias findings. Player 1 has since confirmed coupled loadout BiS scoring and canonical skill headings as deliberate differences.

The authored picker has the main two-map workflows, durable shared state, invitations, RuneMetrics review/apply, and equipment and training views. **Finish the task and migration repairs before switching.** Both maps' unlocked and selected chunks match, but current duties still differ: QXA has 18 original versus 24 app active tasks, and QXB has 8 versus 52. Fix the Prayer recipe and Slayer source filters, Player 2's achievement-tier pruning and zero-Prayer shield goals, and the verified InsidePOH restriction gap; repair or explicitly review the unmatched progress aliases. Most other original-only rows are deliberate F2P corrections, global area utilities, backlog, shared satisfaction or generated skill mirrors. Exact counts and thirty examples per class appear below.

## Method and source boundaries

This review targets branch `codex/parity` at `e023ba86d4e53f4aa61c7613b6b85febc982765f`. It combines executable authored and retained original source, checked-in cache/wiki correction evidence, copied duo saves, and captured original outputs. The retained original is v6.9.68 at `98a3bdc`; actual original clients also reported v6.9.68. Original active tasks were recomputed through the audited noDisplay builder; initial display is retained separately. The final headless capture also read the original worker's existing rejection model without pausing or rerunning it. Static feature presence and checked-in game evidence do not certify deployed-device behavior, actual inventory, every game fact, or complete upstream equivalence.

Sources include [README](../../README.md), [PRODUCT](../../PRODUCT.md), [original-sync](../original-sync.md), [migration](../migration.md), [task coverage](../task-engine-coverage.md), [identity](../../app/tasks/identity.mjs), [migration code](../../app/tasks/migration.mjs), [engine](../../app/tasks/engine.mjs), [presentation](../../app/tasks/presentation.mjs), [equipment](../../app/tasks/equipment.mjs), [settings](../../app/data/settings.mjs), and retained [original index](https://github.com/source-chunk/chunk-picker-rs3/blob/98a3bdc5e0992616ca52c8d2429e7ef2f4fdd8f0/index.js)/[worker](https://github.com/source-chunk/chunk-picker-rs3/blob/98a3bdc5e0992616ca52c8d2429e7ef2f4fdd8f0/worker.js). `docs/tasks.md` does not exist. Source references below are relative to the repository root; line numbers identify the audited snapshot. The canonical Desktop vault router and RS3 context pack were read, with later confirmed PRODUCT decisions taking precedence over the October 4 pack. Private raw captures stay in ignored `.local/research`; the copied `.local/duo` is deleted after evaluation. The exhaustive register in this report is the committed classification record.

The applicable duo policy is explicit: each player's own chunks create her or his obligations; connected partner chunks provide training and supplies for convenience; either player can satisfy a chunk requirement; actual quests, skills, achievements, rewards and usable teleports remain personal; and the duo stays F2P (`PRODUCT.md:26–30`). A shared challenge check never establishes the partner's personal completion or possession.

Duo-loss ratings estimate the current F2P pair’s likely cost, based on active settings and documented product decisions; custom/manual tools are conditional on actual use. For the inventory, **present** means executable code implements the stated bounded feature; **partial** means it exists with a material original-mode or semantic gap; **missing** means an original workflow lacks an equivalent authored consumer. A disabled original feature is labelled separately. Size estimates are **S** for a narrow consumer/UI/metadata change, **M** for several coupled consumers with focused comparisons, and **L** for substantial state or graph design with broad validation.

## Feature inventory

Current source supersedes several older audit claims: Pick for me, unpick reselection, invitation revocation, RuneMetrics review/apply, the training planner UI, and equipment weights are present. Weight evidence covers 3,019 of 3,550 retained equipment rows. These corrections prevent rebuilding already delivered features, while the table preserves their limits.

Invitations support the current trusted duo: a non-administrator invitation can edit both maps, while administrators additionally manage invitations and whole-store replacement. Map selection changes the view, not permissions; an invitation label attributes history without proving who held the link. Consequently, trusted-duo invitations are **not public read-only map sharing**. Public/view-only maps, a friends workflow, proper identity accounts and a true single shared-map group mode remain separate work (`docs/sharing.md`, `app/storage/invites.mjs`, `PRODUCT.md:56–60`). The combined current display correctly represents the duo's separate maps.

## Effective ON rules and reviewed behavior

Fresh headless view-only captures at `2026-10-09T01:04:46.181Z` (October 8 local time) confirmed both clients report v6.9.68 and have the same **35 effective true keys** below, unchanged from the earlier capture. This table describes current authored consumers and their limits, rather than claiming all current task outputs match.

For `category-gate`, the number is the retained catalogue's declared matching tag count, **not** current available goals. The common authored guard is `app/tasks/engine.mjs:228–233` and `:484–491`; the upstream guard is `reference/original-picker/worker.js:4132–4137`. Declared outputs then require ordinary source/requirement checks. Membership can make enabled members-only families irrelevant in the present F2P evaluation. There is no reason to synthesize more obligations merely because a category name exists.

Catalogue classification totals are 15 implemented-scope, 15 category-gate, 2 partial and 3 retained-only. Source review moves **Show Diary Tasks**, **BIS Skilling** and **Boosting** from implemented-scope to partial, yielding **12 implemented-scope, 15 category-gate, 5 partial, 3 retained-only**. `BIS Skilling Relics` retains the catalogue's partial badge while separating its additional planning limitation from established upstream behavior. None of these totals is a count of passing original-feature workflows.


The metadata totals describe declared capability coverage. The reviewed totals describe the consumers found in source, including gaps hidden by current combinations of enabled flags. Neither total proves identical obligation output. In the current class-A audit, no row has a source-proven cause of a missing ON-rule consumer: the current concrete failures instead involve the OFF crafted-item restriction and misinterpreted source metadata. That result does not waive the documented Achievement/Diary, Boosting or skilling-gate control differences.

### OFF rules can still affect current outputs

Both maps also capture `Rare Drop=false`, `Rare Drop Amount="5000"`, `Secondary Primary=false`, `Secondary Primary Amount="1"`, and `Kill X Amount="1"`. Their controls/meaning need inspection even though they are not ON boolean flags. The original rare predicate is strict above 1/5000 and unknown rates bypass rarity; current authored parser shares strict rare admission by default and keeps unknown rates nonprimary. The authored separate boolean controls differ from the original amount-only UI. Secondary primary comparison and normalized zero semantics are documented compatibility corrections (`app/tasks/rates.mjs`, audit Q4/Q5, `docs/parity.md`).

Important current OFF modes: `Highest Level`, `Multi Step Processing`, `InsidePOH`, `Wield Crafted Items`, `Wield Crafted Items Override`, `RDT`, `Primary Spawns`, `Show Diary Tasks Any`, `Puro-Puro`, `Skilling Pets`, `Skillcape` and `Master skillcape`. The InsidePOH gap now has current output evidence: the original rejects clay fireplace and shabby rug under `InsidePOH Primary` when `InsidePOH=false` and level >1 (`reference/original-picker/worker.js:4153–4155`). The app exempts that tag from eligibility and only removes primary training contribution, so it offers higher workbench recipes; clay fireplace is active on both maps. This needs a restriction consumer or an explicit change of rules. Broader multistep production, task-output RDT/Legends guards, telegrab-primary-rune distinction and Puro-Puro source-versus-area semantics remain further comparison targets. Dynamic pet/cape and crafted-item-override omissions are chiefly later-mode relevance because their generating flags are currently false.


The class-A evidence makes one OFF-rule concern concrete: `Wield Crafted Items=false` rejects task-produced inputs for Prayer crafting and urn recipes, even though upstream generated cross-skill copies retain them. This is not merely a future crafted-equipment mode. Turning that flag on only in a detached Player 1 snapshot makes all six affected recipes available with own obligations; no saved state was changed. Other OFF-mode limits above remain comparison targets unless a current row establishes their effect.

## Prioritized switchover checklist

The checklist separates verified current defects and acceptance work from broader original parity. **Blocker** means it must be resolved or explicitly accepted before claiming this duo can switch with the intended obligations; **should** means valuable compatibility or maintenance work without a demonstrated current blocking row; **nice/later** covers convenience or future product scope. A deferred control must be described accurately rather than represented by an implemented badge.

| Priority | Size | Work and acceptance condition | Current evidence / effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| Blocker | S | Separate output acquisition channel from required-item source constraints. Keep `AllowedSources` and `NonShop` handling intentional. Confirm Slay a gelatinous abomination accepts the accessible shop gauntlets on both maps. | Two class-A records. `Source=drop` describes the task output but `app/tasks/engine.mjs:407` wrongly applies it to required Spiked gauntlets; free own Slayer Equipment supply and reachable monster already exist. |
| Blocker | M | Narrow the crafted-item source filter for crafting/urn activities or deliberately reproduce upstream mirror eligibility. Verify the six Prayer recipes on both maps while preserving crafted-equipment/training restrictions. | Twelve class-A records. `app/tasks/engine.mjs:403` filters all Prayer processing inputs. Detached rule counterfactual proves the cause; some recipes already have challenge credit, which does not repair source eligibility. |
| Blocker | M | Implement original same-BaseQuest diary child/tier pruning while preserving highest skill requirements. | QXB has 27 extra active diary children, including Belter Smelter and And It Was THIS Big!; both clients have Show Diary Tasks Complete/Any OFF. This is a current goal-selector gap, separate from general Achievement toggle semantics. |
| Blocker | S | Exclude zero-prayer components from Prayer-specific obtain goals while keeping legitimate tank/Ranged comparisons. | Four QXB shield rows; Blue dragonhide shield is active solely as a Prayer shield despite bonus 0. Original prayer candidates require positive bonus. |
| Blocker | S | Implement `InsidePOH=false` workbench task suppression independently from `InsidePOH Primary` training contribution, or explicitly accept changing that rule. | Clay fireplace and shabby rug create four extra model rows; clay fireplace is active on both maps. Original rejection diagnostics and the exact Level>1 source guard confirm the cause. |
| Blocker | M | Repair eight verified progress aliases; review five conflicting/skill-clone mappings with Player 1 before projecting them. | Six QXB lodestones and the old runite crossbow decoder plus QXA's fresh Constitution portent check need mapping fixes. Quest skill clones require a decision. Preserve originals and never convert challenge checks into personal quest completion or usable teleports. |
| Decision before parity claim | S | Choose coupled-loadout versus original greedy slot scoring, and whether independent Rune arrow ammo remains an obligation. | QXA's six extra Ranged BiS candidate goals are supported by admitted source tasks. Original ammo-dependent accuracy/special weighting (`reference/original-picker/worker.js:6114–6120`) differs from app paired-loadout weighting (`equipment.mjs:264–268`). Free Rune arrow remains in the Acadia longbow tradeoff; Yew shortbow is members-only. |
| Decision before parity claim | S | Accept canonical skill ownership, or restore the current original Constitution portent heading/goal behavior. Keep the three active generated mirror correspondences reviewable. | Original worker copies `Skills` rows into other headings (`reference/original-picker/worker.js:3570–3605`). Canonical rows are available; QXB's two diary counterparts are active. QXA's Divination portent counterpart is suppressed by the current skill-goal selector. |
| Blocker for switch acceptance | M | Re-run this harness after repairs; resolve or explicitly accept remaining unexplained active/highest duties, and document deliberate F2P, partner, backlog and scoring changes. | Each map has three unresolved active goals: String a diamond amulet, Cast Lvl-4 Enchant on a diamond amulet, and Create a dreadfowl pouch. These are six map-active occurrences; only the two Dreadfowl occurrences are active-only supplements. Extra dormant catalogue candidates alone are not blockers. |
| Operational acceptance | M | Exercise two-user conflict/reconnect, save/undo, backup/restore and invitation revocation on a throwaway copied store, then confirm Player 2's intended device/link before retiring the original workflow. | Automated durability/conflict/restore/invite tests pass. This audit did not use the live picker or alter Tailscale; second-device operation remains an acceptance check rather than an unbuilt feature. |
| Should | S | Update stale docs and coverage badges. Distinguish metadata declarations, source-reviewed behavior and captured output; show the retained-only Achievement/lowercase alias and bounded Diary/Boosting/skilling consumers accurately. | Prevents source-proven features being called absent and semantic gaps being called equivalent. Catalogue 15/15/2/3 becomes reviewed 12/15/5/3. |
| Should | M | Restore Achievement versus Show Diary Tasks eligibility/display semantics, and Show Diary Tasks Any when that workflow is required. Test flag combinations and tier grouping rather than only both currently ON. | Achievement has no authored consumer. Both ON mask basic visibility mismatch; no current class-A row is proven missing solely from this control gap. |
| Should; blocker if operative imported records require it | M | Review custom numeric requirements, forced existing tasks, manual item/source edits, randomLoot and discretionary XP allocations; implement equivalent consumers/editors or explicitly show unsupported effects in migration preview. | Raw preservation in portable backup does not prove every imported gameplay effect executes. Custom rows and several legacy source/XP declarations currently have partial consumers. |
| Should | M–L | Compare specialist BiS goal/ranking alternatives, processing/Highest Level chains and shared boost eligibility/ranking against relevant original examples. Fix specific obligation differences before enabling affected modes. | Set effects and coupled weight layouts remain limited; opaque processing chains and XP boost formulas have source-reviewed gaps. An absent relic energy optimizer is not itself a demonstrated upstream parity deficit. |
| Should | M | Add independent personal-level Slayer and full-scroll clue-chance calculators if they are part of the expected original workflow. | Existing Activity Info uses reachable ceilings and declared steps; those do not answer the same personal assignment/chance questions. |
| Should | M–L | Extend one-chunk previews into a disposable multi-step sandbox with discard, if original testMode exploration is required. | Current hypothetical unlock preview is useful but cannot model arbitrary temporary map/rule/progress edits. |
| Should for broader parity | M–L | Implement dynamic skilling-pet and Max/Master max cape generation with exact source/category semantics. | Relevant generating flags are currently OFF, and current F2P switch evidence does not make these the dominant gap. |
| Nice | S–M | Add screenshot/export, mobile long-press unlock, selected-neighbour materialization/sorting and original intro/help/topbar conveniences. | Improves familiar operation and sharing without resolving current source/task defects. Original hiscores are disabled, so restoring them is not required to match an enabled workflow. |
| Later product work | L | Add public read-only sharing, verified identities, general group membership and true single shared-map mode according to explicit product decisions. | Trusted invitations already serve the present editable two-map duo; they do not deliver these separate capabilities. |

## Measured task parity

Final capture: **2026-10-08 22:04:46 Atlantic** (2026-10-09T01:04:46.181Z), confirming the earlier 21:41:54 capture. Copied authored state revision **33**, SHA-256 `e4b739b847c73743390a471693f5830563b11009ba13fe8e7ac481c2c4d256ad`. The normal task scheduler and current cache/shop/equipment corrections evaluated the hydrated copy without opening the writable store. Verified main-checkout geometry contains 6,744 components; map assets are presentation-only. The already-applied gameplay migration was reused without warnings. No app repairs were made in this audit.

The actual original site is [source-chunk.github.io/chunk-picker-rs3](https://source-chunk.github.io/chunk-picker-rs3/), with [QXA view-only](https://source-chunk.github.io/chunk-picker-rs3/?qxa-view) and [QXB view-only](https://source-chunk.github.io/chunk-picker-rs3/?qxb-view), discovered through repository capture/source documentation. Public Firebase is [chunkpickerrs3.firebaseio.com](https://chunkpickerrs3.firebaseio.com/). All browser loads used fresh headless profiles, without credentials or clicks. Firebase HTTP mutations and GET long-poll writes were blocked; the socket whitelist allowed only reads/unlisten/heartbeat. Each accepted map capture made 38 read subscriptions and 37 unlistens; one statistics frame was blocked, no HTTP mutation was attempted, and no worker errors were reported. No live application or Tailscale endpoint was accessed.

The deployed index SHA-256 was `8e4d21bbfad09de8fffe263f131e3f5bdb63fca65da27c27f9e3d670623af894`, matching the retained client after LF normalization. The same-URL worker source GET hash was `76a962701fd22c65d5c78e110dd7eb8733fc365e316e46e431d3d41700343404`; that is source provenance, not a hash intercepted from the executed worker response.

These checks compare the fresh original model against the copied authored arrays, separately from retained import provenance. Claude’s prior sync left unlocked/selected chunks aligned. They do not establish equality of every setting, personal fact, annotation or progress alias.

### What the list counts mean

Both originals have at least 100 unlocked chunks. Their startup path displays saved active challenges until “Show New Tasks”; QXA’s saved list contains 17 rows and freshly computed output contains 18, adding the crawling-hand goal. QXB stays at 8. The harness captures the initial rendered DOM, then calls only the source-verified `setupCurrentChallenges(tempChallengeArrSaved,true,true)` noDisplay branch. It computes the original active model and detached disabled-checkbox HTML without saving, changing challenge colors or clicking the update control (`reference/original-picker/index.js:4218–4230`, original builder; synthetic no-save test). The figures below use that fresh original computation.

**A** means original-only identity in the stated set; **B** means app-only; **C** means an exactly resolved task has different availability, required level or effective boosted level. C overlaps A/B and must not be added to their totals. Matching uses the category-qualified identity registry, historical codes and migration decoder. Unresolved/generated skill mirrors stay unresolved in raw counts; a separately evidenced canonical counterpart is explained rather than silently reassigned.

The full/possible figure is the original worker’s `globalValids` model, not a claim that every row is a displayed or physically doable duty. It includes false collapsed Quest/Diary prerequisites and global `UnlocksArea` utility sentinels. App “eligible” is its own available obligation catalogue, including lower equipment candidates that need not become active. Active goals are the comparable current work list.

| Map / set | Original | App | Exact common | A | B | Exact unresolved original | C |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| QXA active | 18 | 24 | 1 | 17 | 23 | 1 | — |
| QXA full model / eligible | 1536 | 1540 | 1299 | 237 | 241 | 25 | 211 |
| QXB active | 8 | 52 | 3 | 5 | 49 | 2 | — |
| QXB full model / eligible | 1519 | 1584 | 1284 | 235 | 300 | 51 | 182 |

The original has 234 false Quest rows in QXA and 203 false Quest plus 62 false Diary rows in QXB. Excluding these leaves **1,302 / 1,254 positive original admissions**. Against the app’s same full eligible catalogue, positive exact common is **1,065 / 1,019**, A stays **237 / 235**, and B becomes **475 / 565**. Those asymmetric counts expose prerequisite-collapse differences; they do not mean hundreds of new mandatory grinds. All C findings concern positive original admissions.

Original active A consists of 19 local challenge-satisfaction suppressions (16 QXA, 3 QXB) plus three generated skill-heading mirrors with available canonical tasks (QXA Constitution portent; QXB Magic “Blast and Hellfire” and Runecrafting “Not Waving But Drowning”). No active A row proves an entire canonical obligation disappeared. Shared credit is an explicit duo policy; some checks are self/both-player evidence, so this report does not attribute every suppressed goal to Player 2. Canonical skill presentation still needs a decision, especially the portent goal.

Fifteen exact category projections need review: eight fix, five decision, two canonical equivalents already satisfied. Thirteen source rows remain in retained legacy data; the fresh QXA portent check was absent from that retained copy. Preservation is not functional projection. In particular, six QXB lodestone checks need metadata-backed Extra→Diary aliases; they are challenge checks and must not automatically become usable-teleport confirmations. Perils of Ice Mountain step 1 has complete skill clones and a conflicting Construction backlog; Beneath Cursed Tides skill clones are requirement evidence, not automatic personal quest completion. The old runite crossbow `(u)` label needs a canonical rename decoder.

### Current BiS

Compare obtainable recommendations with the original `highestOverall`, separately from personally recorded obtained gear. A challenge check does not prove inventory.

| Map | Original style/slot assignments | Exact | Equivalent ties | Different assignments |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| QXA | 38 | 31 | 3 | 4 |
| QXB | 36 | 34 | 2 | 0 |

QXA’s four differences describe one ranged loadout: original Yew shortbow / Rune arrow / no off-hand / two-hand versus app Rune throwing axe / Off-hand rune crossbow / Rune bolts / dual wield. Pinned Yew shortbow item 857 is members-only and is explicitly excluded locally; the free Rune arrow remains in the **Acadia longbow** two-hand tradeoff. This is a justified source correction plus a coupled-goal decision, not four missing equipment items. Three Team-16→Team-1 cape choices are equal-score ties with 49 alternatives. QXB’s Rune mace→Rune battleaxe and Explorer’s ring 4→ring 1 are equivalent ties; other existing assignments match. Both maps gain 10 Necromancy slot assignments under corrected F2P trial support.

A separate QXB defect generates zero-prayer shields as Prayer goals: Blue dragonhide shield, Mystic shield, Rune kiteshield and Rune square shield. The Rune mace supplies all four prayer points; armour tie-breaking makes a zero-bonus shield a loadout component, then emits it as a Prayer obtain duty. Blue dragonhide shield is active. The original requires each Prayer candidate to have positive prayer bonus (`reference/original-picker/worker.js:6324–6325`); the local component emission needs that guard (`app/tasks/equipment.mjs:298–328`).

## Difference classification and thirty examples per class

“Nothing” means keep the evidenced deliberate correction or representation. “Decision” means Player 1 must choose the expected obligation/presentation; it does not authorize a save change. “Fix” includes investigating an unresolved cause before accepting the extra active grind. These are audit findings, with no gameplay implementation changes in this branch.

### A — original shows it, app does not

The union of active/model A contains **253 QXA + 238 QXB = 491 map/category identities** (the model alone is 237 + 235). Each occurrence is classified; 475 need nothing, 14 need the two eligibility fixes, and two are representation/BiS decisions. No A row is proven to originate solely from an unimplemented ON-rule consumer. Membership exclusions use exact item/source/cache or pinned wiki evidence; they should not be restored to match old totals.

### B — app shows it, original does not

The raw model comparison contains **241 QXA + 300 QXB = 541 map rows**, plus **32 active-only supplements** absent only from original active selection. Of model B, **75** repair original F2P trial/Combat/Necromancy gates, **48** follow reviewed cache/membership corrections, **350** are rule/catalogue differences (288 lower equipment candidates, eight optional Skill Guide objectives, three direct partner-only supply rows, 45 partner-prerequisite access chains, six coupled-scorer differences), **eight** are bugs (four InsidePOH and four zero-Prayer shield rows), and **60** remain source/access gaps needing investigation. The active-only supplements include **27 QXB diary-child bugs**, **three equivalent tie choices** and **two Dreadfowl frontier gaps**. The final worker diagnostics resolved 55 previously uncertain rows. For 18 remaining rows, an exact rejected input/subsection is captured but its underlying source-closure difference is unproven; another 42 model rows and two active frontier rows have no rejection entry explaining the absence. These are explicitly labelled low/medium confidence, not invented data corrections.

Original worker `nonValids` is flat by raw task label, not category. The harness read 18,306 QXA and 18,336 QXB rejection entries from existing lexical worker state using a no-pause DevTools attachment. The original active identities, full model, chunks, progress, rules and BiS remained stable. A missing diagnostic entry is not proof the original admitted that goal or that either engine is correct.

The diary defect is separate from switching either flag off: both Show Diary Tasks Complete and Show Diary Tasks Any are false in both clients. The original still collapses same-BaseQuest children when later parent/tier goals are valid (`reference/original-picker/worker.js:532–571,1839–1865`). App presentation does not implement that pruning (`app/tasks/presentation.mjs:147–150`). Player 2 gets “Belter Smelter”, “And It Was THIS Big!”, “Blast and Hellfire” and other tier children as extra duties. Preserve original skill-highest mirrors while repairing diary-parent grouping; do not blindly suppress all Combat Mastery groups.

## Reusing the read-only harness

Run from an isolated worktree with a newly copied save under that worktree’s `.local`. Copy the whole store so checksummed static sidecars are available; remove copied `store.lock` and `invites.json` before evaluation. The harness never reads access tokens/invitations, opens the writable store, starts a picker server or imports progress into an original map. Delete the copied store after the audit. `.local/research` output is ignored and must not be committed; it contains gameplay evidence.

```powershell
node scripts/research/original-parity.mjs `
  --data-dir .local/duo `
  --game-data "C:/Users/User/Projects/chunk-picker-rs3/.local/game-data" `
  --map-assets "C:/Users/User/Projects/chunk-picker-rs3/.local/map-assets" `
  --output .local/research/original-parity-review.json
```

`--capture-only` captures the original without a save copy. `--original-capture .local/research/prior-capture.json` replays captured original output offline against a copy; it makes no original-service requests. `--maps qxa,qxb` is the authorized default; other maps are rejected. Use `--chrome PATH` for a Chrome binary override. Existing output is never overwritten. Browser profiles use ephemeral safe localhost debug ports and are removed after the owned Chrome child exits. Source hash changes stop the audited no-save recomputation until reviewed. The worker rejection diagnostic is optional and its availability is recorded.

`local-parity-model.mjs` hydrates and checksum-verifies static fields, builds the normal production world/scheduler, and resolves exact original identities via the app’s registry/migration code. It preserves saved chunk arrays, original category identities, required/effective levels, canonical saved statuses and obtainable BiS separately. It does not fuzz-match labels or turn challenge checks into personal facts.

Validation: final complete `npm test` passed **1,776 tests**, **one skipped, zero failed** (1,777 total). All **26** new focused research tests passed. An offline end-to-end replay of the final original capture against the copied store reproduced every reported count after the last harness edits. Focused tests execute the retained noDisplay builder with save/display functions forbidden; exercise Firebase HTTP/socket/long-poll mutation guards, binary/malformed frames and spoofed hosts; reject unsafe output paths; preserve identity and independent progress sets; and prevent BiS tie alternatives crossing equipment slots. Both client and worker source pins are checked. The first suite attempt had two Windows ENOBUFS localhost socket-allocation failures; affected tests and subsequent full suites passed without app changes.

The copied state hash remained unchanged after all reads/evaluations. The copied `.local/duo` (including its copied token, backups and history) was removed after evaluation; no private save/capture JSON was staged. Every fresh original-browser scratch profile was removed after its owned Chrome process exited. No main tracked files, main duo store, live picker or Tailscale configuration were changed.

The following grouped register classifies every A union identity, every B model or active-only identity, and every C drift. A row may occur in several sets/classes; these are **not disjoint totals**. Maps sharing the same exact category/identity/cause are combined; map-specific causes remain separate. A/B sets are marked `active`, `model`, or both. Exact IDs retain unresolved mirror identities for review. This is the committed classification record; private raw capture JSON is not required to read it.

<details>
<summary>B: coupled-vs-ammo-dependent-bis-scoring — 6 map rows</summary>

| Maps | Category / named task | Identity | Set | Class; action | Root cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| QXA | BiS: Obtain a Rune throwing axe | rs3:goal:equipment:000aa6 | model | rule_group_difference; decision | Original truthy Wield/source tasks prove the Ranged equipment route is already available. Original reference/original-picker/worker.js:6114–6120 excludes accuracy from greedy per-slot scores when a main-hand thrown weapon needs no ammunition and halves its special bonus. Authored equipmentPower/describe scores main accuracy and full main bonus in compatible complete loadouts, then applies hand weighting. This changes the QXA selected loadout from Yew shortbow/Rune arrow to Rune throwing axe + off-hand peers + Rune bolts. This is an explicit scoring-semantic difference; it is not a newly discovered item source. |

| Maps | Category / named task | Identity | Set | Class; action | Root cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| QXA, QXB | Summoning: Create a dreadfowl pouch | rs3:task:001c69 | active only | unsupported_unresolved; fix | Original possible already admits Create a dreadfowl pouch at4, but original highestCurrent has no Summoning goal and highestOverall.Summoning is Create a spirit wolf pouch{0}. Fresh worker diagnostics has no rejection entry for dreadfowl. Authored selects the available level4 pouch. The original frontier suppression remains untraced; membership and Show Skill Tasks do not differ. The bounded source trace narrows this to the training frontier: original admitted pouch metadata is Primary=true, Secondary=true; checkPrimaryMethod requires a non-secondary starter, then highest selection gates on primary training or passive/manual access. Authored independently reports primaryMethod=false, training.primary=false and passiveLevel=4, while its presentation ranks the metadata primary flag. The original passive/quest-XP runtime inputs were not retained, so the precise selector versus passive-access cause remains unproven. |

</details>
